> **Dispatch (Cowork writing side, begun 2026-11-08, the sitting that booted on
> `records/cowork/handoff/cowork_handoff_entry_six_hundred_and_three.md`).** Executes **step 2 of the L3 route ruling** —
> `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_02_l3_route_sitting.md` §2, lines 59–61: *"One Claude Code dispatch,
> the application act, re-marks the ruled rows in the reading file and writes the amended derived L3 specification as a
> NEW file beside the derivation, whose body is not rewritten and which gains D-674's top banner only."* Its shape is the
> L2 application act's, `records/cc/instructions/cc_instruction_l2_application_2026_10_10.md`; **everything this batch
> needs from that dispatch is stated here, and you are not sent to read it** (Ruling 2 of
> `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_02_boot_chain_sitting.md`, item 2).
>
> **★ WHAT THIS BATCH IS, AND WHAT IT IS NOT.** It APPLIES rulings already taken. The L3 family records of 2026-11-02 to
> 2026-11-08 hand their writing to this act in their own words; what they hand is gathered, each item at its record and
> line, in `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_application_carry_list_2026_11_08.md` (the carry list), and §L below

## Words used in this dispatch, explained first

- **The derivation** — the blind derivation of L3: the L3 specification written by a session that had not seen the old
  design documents. Its statements are numbered L3-S1 to L3-S42 [relayed from the application surface's words section;
  ★ re-taken 2026-11-09 by the sitting booted on entry 634 at the derivation's bold statement headings, L3-S1 to L3-S42
  each met once, in order]. Its file name, read at the object 2026-11-09 by the same sitting (named at lines 17 and 35
  of `records/cc/instructions/cc_instruction_l3_derivation_commit_2026_10_17.md`, then staged by that path, 127,533
  bytes): **`cowork_blind_derivation_l3_2026_10_17.md`**, at the repository root. *(Former wordings, kept (#12): "the
  count not re-taken by this sitting"; "Its file name is **[AT RELEASE]**, read at the object.")* It is a dated record
  of that act; under D-674 its body is never rewritten.
- **The successor** — the new file this batch writes beside the derivation: the derived L3 specification as amended by
  the L3 family rulings, **NOT RATIFIED**. Its file name, authored 2026-11-09 (the sitting booted on entry 634) on the
  pattern of the L2 successor (`cowork_derived_specification_l2_2026_10_10.md`, named after its dispatch's date), and
  ratified as written by the user
  (`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_13_l3_application_release_part_b_sitting.md`, Ruling 5) (former
  wording, kept (#12): "and overrulable by the user before Task 0"): **`cowork_derived_specification_l3_2026_11_08.md`**,
  at the repository root
  beside the derivation. *(Former wording, kept (#12): "Its file name is authored when Task 1 is written and named on
  this dispatch's face so the user can overrule it before Task 0.")*
- **The reading file** — `ratification_surfaces/cowork_comparison_l3_reading.md`: the table comparing every *outgoing statement*
  of the population its §3 defines (one per row of its §6) against the derivation *(corrected at release, step 5 part 5, 2026-11-22, by the sitting booted on entry 1163, under finding 59 of `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_application_release_check_2026_11_16.md`, with one departure in punctuation, the term's italics kept; former wording, kept (#12): "every statement of the old L3 design documents (an outgoing statement, one per row of its §6)")*. Each row
  carries one *disposition* from the closed vocabulary of its §5. A row split into claims is cited `Row m.n (i)`.
- **A ruled line** — ONE line ADDED immediately beneath a row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph, opening
  `*RULED DISPOSITION (`, naming the record file and ruling, then the disposition and the placement in the ruling's
  words (form (b)). The proposed paragraph is never edited (#12).
- **A recorded line** — the same, opening `*RECORDED DISPOSITION (`, used ONLY where a record states that a row's
  placement follows from the record as this side's derivation and was not ruled (form (d)).
- **An amendment** — a change a record orders to a derived statement, an open question, the charter or an interface
  contract: a status change, a clause written beside a statement, a clause replaced, an open question answered, a
  statement added.
- **The ledger** — §L below: per record, the writing side's transcription of what each ruling orders, as a locator aid.
  **The record's own words govern; where the ledger and the record differ, the record governs and the difference is
  reported.**
- **"Decided against"** — the wording the L3 records use for rows whose content a ruling rejected. They name no
  disposition word for it; Ruling 1 of the surface sitting (ruling ledger below) supplies it: HISTORICAL.
- **A changed passage** — a run of lines a diff shows as changed together (the stretch under one `@@ … @@` line of
  git's own diff output). Where a task names its changed passages by their sites, count by site.

## The forms, fixed here so the batch takes no decision

**(a) Amendments in the successor** (the L2 application dispatch's form (a), adapted, with the forms for a rewritten statement, an added statement and the lists placed under the successor's *Notes* heading added *(corrected at release, step 5 part 5, 2026-11-22, by the sitting booted on entry 1162, under finding 60 of `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_application_release_check_2026_11_16.md`, with one departure in wording, "the lists placed under the successor's Notes heading" for its "the notes lists"; former wording, kept (#12): "stated here whole")*):
- A **status change**: the *Status* field is edited; the former value preserved in the same field as
  `(was: "<former>"; <record file>, Ruling N)`.
- A **falsifier clause replaced**: the *Falsifier* field edited, the former clause preserved in the same field as
  `(was: "<former clause>"; <record file>, Ruling N)`.
- A **Defense clause replaced**: the *Defense* field edited at that clause only, the former clause preserved in the
  same field in the same `(was: …)` form.
- A **statement rewritten** by a record's words (the records name L3-S7, L3-S24, L3-S28 and the stale pointer of
  L3-S5 (ii), carry list §4): the field the record names is edited to the record's words, the former wording preserved
  in the same field in the `(was: …)` form. Where the record gives no exact wording, that amendment is a
  STOP-and-report, the rest applied.
- A **clause beside a statement**: a sub-item beneath the six fields, `- *Beside L3-Sn (<record file>, Ruling N).*` +
  the record's words quoted.
- A statement that **stands as derived** by a ruling's words: a sub-item `- *Ruled (<record file>, Ruling N).* Stands as
  derived.` — no text change. By a record's own derivation and not by a ruling: `- *Recorded (<record file>,
  §<section>).* Stands as derived — recorded by the record as this side's derivation, not ruled; challengeable.` + the
  record's words quoted.
- An open question **answered**: it stays where it stands, gaining `**ANSWERED (<record file>, Ruling N):**` and the
  record's answering sentence quoted beneath it.
- **A statement ADDED by a ruling** (the records order statements *"to be added to the amended L3 specification"*:
  display four, architecture rules six, span kinds three, literature facts one, the chord-span pair one, the bass role
  per event one — carry list §5; and, under Ruling 2 above, the statements *"the derivation"* is *"to gain"*, carry list
  §4 item 5; and the suggestion-tool record's boundary statement, which the carry list's §5 has named since 2026-11-09 (its lines 127–130; §L.39's *Open* point (4)) (former wording, kept (#12): "which the carry list does not name (§L.39)") *(★ added
  2026-11-09 at the check turn, the sitting booted on entry 639, read at §L.39 line 2849 of this dispatch as it stood on 2026-11-09 (locate it by its `### §L.` heading; the words from "as it stood" to here added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1074, on the release check's finding 39; nothing replaced); the record
  itself not opened at this check)*): written in a section of the successor headed `## Statements added by the L3 family rulings`, after the
  last derived statement, each headed `### Added — <record file>, Ruling N, <part as the record names it>` and carrying
  the record's text quoted whole. **No L3-S number is allocated to an added statement and no derived statement is
  renumbered.** Where the record gives no text, only a subject, that addition is a STOP-and-report, the rest applied.
  *Authored by the writing side 2026-11-08 (the L2 form allowed no added statement; the L3 records order them);
  ratified as written by the user
  (`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_13_l3_application_release_part_b_sitting.md`, Ruling 1). (Former
  wording, kept (#12): "Authored by the writing side 2026-11-08 (the L2 form allowed no added statement; the L3
  records order them); the user may overrule it before Task 0.")*
- **Lists for the successor's notes** (the records mark them *"the writing side's proposal as put … not a ruled act"*,
  carry list §5): written in a section headed `## Notes — lists the records place here, not ruled`, each list under
  its record's name, quoted, carrying the record's own *"not a ruled act"* words. *Authored 2026-11-08; ratified as
  written by the user (`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_13_l3_application_release_part_b_sitting.md`,
  Ruling 2). (Former wording, kept (#12): "Authored 2026-11-08; overrulable before Task 0.")*

**(b) Lines in the reading file:**
`*RULED DISPOSITION (<record file>, Ruling N).* **<disposition>** — <placement in the record's words>.`
A row the record places "as proposed" still gets its line, saying *as proposed*.

**Which ADOPTED word, and when *as proposed* is written.** For each row-part a record places ADOPTED, read the row's
`*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph and its *Current-text axis* for that part; a row with no `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*`
paragraph is a STOP-and-report. Then:
(1) **The record names the word in full** (*ADOPTED — proposed*, or *ADOPTED — carried* with its L3-S number): that word.
(2) **The record names an L3-S number as carrying the content**: **ADOPTED — carried** with that number.
(3) **The record says the content is *"carried by"* an added statement** (form (a)): **ADOPTED — proposed**, the placement
quoting the record's *"carried by …"* words. An added statement has no L3-S number and is not a derived statement. If
the axis for that part names a derived statement, the row is a STOP-and-report.
(4) **The record writes a bare *"ADOPTED"* or *"carried"* and names no L3-S number:** if the row's proposed word for that
part is an ADOPTED word, that word. Otherwise the word is read at the axis for that part: exactly one derived statement
named, **AGREES**, gives **ADOPTED — carried** with its number; **THE DERIVATION IS SILENT** gives **ADOPTED —
proposed**; anything else is a STOP-and-report.
The line says *as proposed* only where its word, and its number if any, are the row's own proposed word for that part,
and the record adopts the content as proposed. Otherwise *as proposed* is not written, and the placement is given in
the record's words. That includes a row proposed UNPLACED, and a ruling that adopts narrower content than the proposal
(§L.27 part E, Rows 38.47 and 38.48; §L.28, Row 36.106 (i)). No other wording (such as *"placed from"*) is added.
Applied row by row; the rows reached are listed in the report under *ADOPTED words read at the row*.
*Added at release by Ruling 9 of
`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md`, the part 2 ruling record,
in its words (its record lines 388–404), after the *as proposed* sentence above, as the ruling orders.*

A row named by two records gets each record's lines, in ledger order, and is listed in the report under *rows named by
more than one record*. The rule is applied by part, a part being what a record's line names: where two records' lines
fall on the same part with differing dispositions, that part is a STOP-and-report and gets neither of those lines;
lines on different parts are each written. A part a record says it moves to another record gets no line from the
record that moves it. (Written at release by Ruling 3 of the part 2 ruling record, in its words (its record lines
108–112), in place of the sentence that stood here; former wording, kept (#12): "**A row named by two records gets both
records' lines, in ledger order, and is listed in the report under *rows named by more than one record*; where the two
lines' dispositions differ, that row is a STOP-and-report and gets neither.**") Where a record's placement of a split row
names only one claim, the line names that claim: `(i) **…**`.

**(b-H) A row a record "decided against"** (Ruling 1 above): form (b) with the disposition **HISTORICAL** and the
placement quoting the record's sentence that decides against it; where the record decides against part of the row, the
line names that part, `(ii) **HISTORICAL** — …`, and the rest of the row keeps what the record says of it. You find
these rows by searching every L3 ruling record of 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-08 for *"decided against"* and *"decides
against"*, every hit read at its line; §L names the rows the writing side found and is not the whole list. A hit you
cannot tie to a row number is listed in the report, nothing written for it.

**(c) A flagged item answered** (a §6 row carrying no `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph, which a record says it
answers): ONE line ADDED beneath the row's last line, `*RULED (<record file>, Ruling N).* Answered — <the record's
sentence>.` No disposition word is written, and the row is not counted in the ruled distribution.

**(c-P) A row-part a ruling places outside every charter** (Ruling 1 of
`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md`, the part 2 ruling record,
in the working list's item (xxxii) wording): ONE line ADDED beneath the row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph,
`*RULED PLACEMENT (<record file>, Ruling N).* Outside every charter — <the record's placement in its words>; listed in
the successor's notes.`, naming the part where the ruling places a part only. No disposition word is written; the row
is not counted in the ruled distribution and is listed in the report as placed outside every charter. This covers the
sites of the working list's items (xxxii) to (xxxvi) and Row 4.17's three chord-name items (§L.16, §L.52). *Added at
release by the sitting booted on entry 1012, as that ruling orders (its record lines 27–37).* The same label stands on the line Ruling 4 of the part 2 ruling record gives Rows 8.23, 8.35 (i) and 8.113 (ii) (§L.12, line 1188), where the line reads *"Held, …"*, not *"Outside every charter — …"*, and the rows are listed in the report as held by ruling (the close's section *held by ruling*), not as placed outside every charter. *(Sentence added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1074, on the release check's finding 34; nothing replaced.)*

**(d) A recorded line:** ONE line ADDED immediately beneath the row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph,
`*RECORDED DISPOSITION (<record file>, §<section>).* **<disposition>** — <as proposed, or the placement in the record's
words>; <the record's ground for the row, in its words>. Recorded by the record as this side's derivation, not ruled;
challengeable at the row.` Rows carrying a recorded line are counted in a column of their own, never added into the
ruled counts. A row one record names by a ruled line and another by a recorded line follows form (b)'s two-record rule.

**(e) Amendments to `FRAMEWORK.md` and to the ratified interface contracts** (carry list §2 and §3): each edit at the
site the record names, the former wording preserved at the same site in the `(was: "<former>"; <record file>, Ruling

## The standing bars, and the commit-and-push discipline

**The standing bars bind this batch whole:** no `src/` edit, no golden, no test changed, moved or run, nothing under
`tools/corpus/` or `tools/robust_stop/`, **no measurement of the analysis built, designed, scoped or run**, no design,
no repair, **no derivation of any specification statement — an amendment is the record's words placed, never this
batch's**, NO SESSION BOOTED, no document archived, moved or deleted as a file, **no open-items row created, flipped or
discarded, except an apparatus-finding row under rule (c) of `CLAUDE.md`'s open-items register section**, no
decisions-register entry and no `D-NNN` allocated (the records route their supersessions through *"the application act
and the register route"*; what this batch writes for them is fixed when §L is written — *★ 2026-11-09, the check turn
(the sitting booted on entry 640): §L is written, and its opening leaves this **[AT RELEASE]**; the words before this
note are the former wording, kept (#12)*; ★ 2026-11-16, release step 3 (the sitting booted on entry 1020): fixed at §L's opening: nothing into `DECISIONS.md`, no `D-NNN`, under Ruling 4 of the first Part B ruling record), **no edit to any governing
document save the ones the Tasks name**, to any outgoing text of the comparison, or to the derivation's BODY — the
derivation gains one top banner and nothing else — **no ruling taken, no position weighed, no verdict on the
derivation or on any open question**, no recommendation anywhere: not in the successor, not in the reading file, not in
the report, not in a commit message.

**Commit-and-push per task: every task's commit is PUSHED to the central repository (`origin`, GitHub) before the next
task begins, and the push is verified at `.git/refs/remotes/origin/master` equalling the new tip.** Verify every commit
at the object by explicit hash. Every shell command carries `; echo "exit:$?"`. Plain single-quoted `git commit -m '…'`
subjects. The reserved-word and vocabulary rules bind every line you write in your own words (a record's words placed under a form keep the record's wording: an amendment is the record's words placed, never this batch's) — *score* only the musical score, *key* only tonality,
*bar* only the metric unit (the prohibiting sense is *exclude*), no numeric grade anywhere; the disposition vocabulary
is the reading file's §5 closed set and nothing else. *(★ release step 5 part 5, 2026-11-22, finding 100 of the release check: the words from "you write in your own words" to "never this batch's)" added; nothing replaced.)*

## Findings in this batch

**A row a record names whose content, read at the row, does not match what the record says of it** (a row number that
names a different outgoing statement than the record describes; a record placing a row under a disposition the reading
file's §5 does not carry; a row two records would place differently) **is a STOP-and-report for THAT ROW**: the row is
left at its proposed disposition, listed in the report under *rows the act could not place* with what was read, and the
rest of the record is applied. An amendment whose target does not carry the wording the ledger quotes as standing is a
STOP-and-report for that amendment, the rest applied. Nothing is repaired, re-read into a different placement, or
placed by this batch's own judgment.

## §L — The ledger, record by record, in sitting order

**How this ledger is ordered and written.** One subsection per L3 ruling record of 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-08, in the
order the sittings took them. That order is taken from each record's modification time in the rulings folder's listing
of 2026-11-08 — a stand-in for the sitting order, which a later correction to a record's file could have moved. The
order matters only for form (b)'s rule on a row named by two records. Each entry is written by the writing side from
its record **read whole**. It is a locator aid: **the record's own words govern** (words section, *The ledger*).

**Where a record does not place its rows by number.** Some early records name a family of rows (for example "the 76
DIFFERS rows of L3-S8") and leave each row's line to this act, saying the act re-reads each row at the reading file's
§6. For those, the entry states the family as the record bounds it, the groups the record names, and the disposition
the record or a ruling supplies for each group. Where neither supplies a disposition word for a group, the entry says
so, and **those rows are STOP-and-report under *Findings*** unless the releasing sitting records otherwise above them.

**The decisions-register supersessions** each record states are listed in its entry under *Register*. What this batch
writes for them is fixed here, the ledger being complete: **nothing into `DECISIONS.md`, and no `D-NNN`**; each entry's *Register* line stays a list, not an instruction; the close restates the lists (Task 5); carrying the supersessions into the register is left to the register route the records hand them to (§L.2, §L.3, §L.4, §L.52), not to this batch.

record: *"**Algorithm: a change.**"* — *"B adds matches made through a substitution mapping and, in each, the
substituted member and its operation. No change to L2."*

run, published as counts), its surface `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_modal_counts_family_surface_2026_11_07.md` (the
record: *"it stands alone and is not reproduced here (#6)"*). **Ruling 1** (the user's word *"Yes"*), *"L3 publishes the
modal counts per key area (CONFIRMED)"*, put as one confirmation after D-599 was applied first, *"Adding an output to L3's
list being the user's"* (its §0). **As ruled** (its lines 33–36): *"For each key area of L2's principal reading (a key
area as the key-areas Ruling 2 defines it), L3 publishes D-276's counts: for each scale degree 1..7, the sounding
duration and onset count of every chromatic inflection of that degree observed in the key area, computed from L1's
published note facts relative to L2's tonic and mode, un-rounded, with their establishment status. No mode label is
published (D-276)."* **This ruling changes the algorithm:** *"**Algorithmic change, decided:** L3 gains one published
fact, the modal counts per key area. Before this ruling L3 published no modal count."*

   - **L3-S7** (§L.14 amendments 2 and 3). The record that ordered the change, in every mark below:

     1. **Body**, text ratified by Ruling 12 (`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_13_l3_application_release_statement_texts_sitting.md`):
        > **L3-S7. At each change of tonality in L2's principal reading, L3 publishes the pivot label as a view: the decided
        > chord of the span on each side of the change, re-expressed as a scale degree in the other tonality, each carrying a
        > flag stating whether that chord is diatonic in both tonalities. The flag is a fact, not a test of pivot-ness. No chord
        > is named "the pivot". Each rival whole reading L2 publishes gives its own set. Under IC-L2 a pivot is published as one
        > span with one tonality and the other tonality's reading as a rival (L2-S19); the label re-expresses L2's decided
        > chords and decides no tonality.**

        Mark: `(was: "<former>"; records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_03_l3_pivot_label_sitting.md, Ruling 1; text ratified, records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_13_l3_application_release_statement_texts_sitting.md, Ruling 12)`.
     2. **Falsifier**, text ratified by Ruling 12 (the same record), replacing the whole derived clause:
        > CODE. **Observable:** L3's output schema, and the pivot-label outputs at each change of tonality in L2's principal
        > reading and in each rival whole reading L2 publishes. **Decision rule:** falsified if any L3 output changes the
        > tonality L2 decided for a span, or publishes a second tonality as decided for a span; if a re-expressed degree is not
        > the decided chord of its span re-expressed in the other tonality; if a diatonic-in-both flag misstates whether that
        > chord is diatonic in both tonalities; if any chord is named "the pivot"; or if a pivot label is published where the
        > reading it belongs to has no change of tonality. **Not falsified by:** the re-expression of each side's decided chord as
        > a scale degree in the other tonality, with its flag — the record's purpose, *"the re-expression is allowed while a
